Statutory orders and notifications issued by the Ministries of the Govern- 
ment of India (other than the Ministry of Defence) and by Central 
Authorities (other than the Administrations of Union Territories). 


MINISTRY OF HOME AFFAIRS 

New Delhi, the 26th September 1970 

S.O. 3406. — In exercise of the powers conferred by clause (2) of article 77 of 
the Constitution, the President hereby make s the following rules further to amend 
the Authentication (Orders and other Instruments) Rules, 1958, namely:— 

1. (1) These rules may be called the Authentication (Orders and other Instru- 
ments) Sixth Amendment Rules, 1970. 

(2) They shall come into force on the date of their publication in the Official 
Gazette. 

2. In the Authentication (Orders and other Instruments) Rules, 1958, 

(a) In rule 2, for the existing entries, the following entries shall he substituted, 
namely : — 

“(1) by the signature of a Secretary, Special Secretary, Additional Secretary, 
Joint Secretary, Deputy Secretary, Under Secretary or Assistant Secre- 
tary to the Government of India; or 

(2) by the signature of a Head of Department as defined in the Delegation of 
Financial Powers Rules, 1958, other than a Head of Department in any 
Union territory; or 


( 4781 ) 





4782 THE GAZETTE of INDIA: OCTOBER 24, 1970/KARTIKA 2 , 1892 [Pa*t II— 


(3) In the case of orders and other instruments relating to a Department, 

Directorate, Commission, Board, Office or an Organisation specified In 
column 1 of the Schedule to these rules, by an officer specified against 
that Department, Directorate, Commission, Board, Office or Organisation 
in column 2 of the said Schedule; or 

(4) in the case pf orders and other instruments relating to the Ministry of 

Education and Youth Services by a Joint Educational Adviser, Deputy 
Educational Adviser, Assistant Educational Adviser or Statistical Officer 
in that Ministry; and in the case of orders and other instrument® 
relating to the Gazetteers Unit of that Ministry by the Editor 
(Gazetteers; or 

(B) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Ministry of 
External Affairs, by a Director, an Attache or Administrative Officer 
in that Ministry; or 

(6) in the case of orders and other instruments relating tc the Ministry of 

Finance (Department of Economic ASairs), by a Director, Controller or 
Deputy Controller of Capital Issues, Joint Director (Stock Exchange) or 
Joint Director (Accounts) or Senior Accounts Officer in that Department; 
or 

(7) In the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Ministry of 

Finance (Department of Expenditure), by a Director, Finance Officer, 
Deputy Finance Officer or Deputy Assistant Financial Adviser in that 
Department; or 

(8) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Ministry of 

Finance (Department of Revenue and Insurance), by the Commissioner 
((Revision Applications) or the Director or Assistant Director, Tax 
Credit (Export) Division, or the Director (Drawbacks) in that Depart- 
ment; or 

(9) in the case of ordeis and other instruments relating to the Ministry of 

Finance (Defence Division), by a Financial Adviser, Additional 
Financial Adviser, Deputy Financial Adviser or Assistant Financial 
Adviser in that Ministry; or 

<10) in the case of cancellation of assignments of policies of life assurance, 
financed from a Provident Fund, which have been assigned in favour 
of the President of India, by the Accounts Officer of the Fund as 
defined in the rules relating to the Fund; or 

(11) In the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Ministry of 

Food, Agriculture, Community Development and Co-operation (Depart- 
ment of Food), by the Chief Director of Purchase, Deputy Chief Director 
of Purchase, Assistant Chief Director of Purchase, a Director or a 
Deputy Director In the Department of Food in that Ministry; or 

(12) In the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Ministry of 

Foreign Trade, by a Director or a Deputy Director (Export Promotion) 
in that Ministry; or 

(13) In the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Office of the 

Chief Controller of Imports and Exports, by the Chief Controller 
of Imports and Exports; or 

(14) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Ministry of 

Health and Family Planning, and Works, Housing and Urban Develop- 
ment, by the Director (Establishment) in the Department of Health; or 

(IB) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Administrative 
Vigilance Unit In the Ministry of Health and Family Planning, and 
Works., Housing and Urban Development (Department of Works, Hous- 
ing and Urban Development), by an Assistant Vigilance Officer in that 
Department and in the case of orders and other instruments relating to 
the Works Division of that Department by a Section Officer In that 
Division; or 

(10) in the case of orders and other Instruments relating to the Ministry 

of Home Affairs, by a Director, a Deputy Director or a Deputy 
Financial Adviser and in the case of orders and other instruments 




SC. 1 ®)'] "nffi GAZETTE OF INDIA: OCTl IER 24 , 1S>70/KAIUTAA 4783 


relating to the Secretariat Security Organisation including Reception 
Wing, by the Chief Security Officer of that Ministry; or 

(17) in the case or orders and other instruments relating to the Ministry 

of Industrial Development and Internal Trade (Department of 
industrial Development), by the Commissioner (Industrial Co- 
operatives) or the Deputy Director (Industrial Cooperatives) in that 
Department or 

(18) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Ministry 

of Irrigation and Power; by the Director (Foreign Exchange and 
Power), Deputy Director (Power) or Budget Officer in that Ministry; 
or 

(19) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Ministry 

of Labour, Employment and Rehabilitation (Department of Rehabili- 
tation), by the Director, Joint Director or Finance and Budget Officer 
in that Department; or 

(20) in the case of authoritative text in Hindi of any order, rule, regulation 

or bye-law issued under the Constitution or under any Central 
Act, where such text is published in Section 8A of Part II of the 
Gazette of India, by an Additional Draftsman, a Deputy Draftsman 
or an Assistant Draftsman, Official Language (Legislative) Com- 
mission, Legislative Department, Ministry of Law; or 

(21) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Ministry 

of Railways, by a Secretary, Additional Secretary, Joint Secretary, 
Deputy Secretary or Under Secretary to, or by a Di rector, Joint 
Director, Deputy Director or Assistant Director of ,the Railway 
Board; or 

(22) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Ministry 

of Supply, by the Director of Vigilance in that Ministry; or 

(28) in the case of demand notices under clause 9 of the General Condi- 
tions of Contract as contained in form Con. 117 or under clause* 
2(d) and (8) of the conditions of Auction Sales prescribed under 
Schedule HI of the Autioneering Contract requesting purchasers to 
pay to the Government any sum of money recoverable from or 
payable by purchasers cm account of either loss on resale or for any 
other reason under a contract or sale entered into by the Directo- 
rate General of Supplies and Disposals, by a Director or a Deputy 
Director of Supplies or a Deputy Director of Disposals, and in the 
case of demand notices for recovery or payment in term* of Arbi- 
tration Awards arising out of Contracts and Supply orders placed 
by the Directorate General of Supplies and Disposals for purchase 
and supply of stores and also in terms of court decrees arising out 
of such contracts and supply orders or otherwise, by a Director, 
Deputy Director or Assistant Director of Supplies; or 

(24) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Depart- 

ment of Atomic Energy, by the Director in that Department; or 

(25) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Depart- 

ment of Communications, by the Deputy Wireless Adviser or the 
Assistant Wireless Adviser; or 

(26) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to Cabinet 

Secretariat (Department of Personnel) by the Chief Welfare Officer 
or a Director in that Department; or 

(27) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to the President’s 

Secretariat, by the Secretary to the President, Deputy Secretary 
to the President or Under Secretary, President’s Secretariat; or 

(28) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Office of 

the Military Secretary to the President, by the Deputy Military 
Secretary to the President or Under Secretary in that Office; or 

(29) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Govern- 

ment Hospitality Organisation, Rashtrapati Bhavan, by the Deputy 
Director General of that Organisation; or

(30) In the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Secretariat 

of the Vice-President, by the Private Secretary or Assistant Private 
Secretary to the Vice-President; or 

(31) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Prime 

Minister’s Secretariat, by the Secretary, the Joint Secretary or the 
Private Secretary to he Prime Minister or the Director in that 
Secretariat; or 

(32) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Election' 

Commission, by the Secretary in that Commission, or 

(33) in the case of orderls and other instruments relating to the Union 

Public Service Commission, by the Secretary, Deputy Secretary-in- 
charge of Administration or Under Secretary-in-charge of Adminis- 
tration in the Office of that Commission; or 

(34) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Secretariat 

of Rajya Sabha (Council of States), by its Secretary, Joint Secretary, 
Deputy Secretary or Under Secretary; or 

(35) in the case of orders and other instruments relating to the Secretariat 

of the Uok Sabha (House of. the People), by its Secretary, Joint 
Secretary, Deputy Secretary or Under Secretary”; 

(b) the following Schedule shall be inserted at the end, namely: — ■

4792 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: OCTOBER 24, 1970/KARTIKA 2, 1892 [Past II— 


New Delhi, the 5th October 1970 

8.0. 3407. — In pursuance of clause (1) of article 239 of the Constitution and in 
supersession of the notifications of the Government of India In the Ministry of 
Home Affairs No. 1/68/60-F'Ill, dated the 22nd June, 1901, and No. 1/25/02-F.III, 
dated the 8th June, 1002, the President hereby directs that in relation to the Union 
territory of Tripura, the District Magistrates, West Tripura, North Tripura, and 
South Tripura, within their respective jurisdictions, shall also discharge the func- 
tions of the Central Government in making orders of the nature specified In 
clauses (a), (b), (c) and (cc) of sub-section (2) of section 3 of the Foreigners Act, 
1940 (31 of 1940), subject to the following conditions, namely:—. 

(a) that the functions so entrusted shall be exercised in respect of nationals 

of Pakistan; 

(b) that In the exercise of such functions, the said District Magistrates shall 

comply with such general or special directions as the Central Govern- 
ment may from time to time issue; and 

(c) that notwithstanding this entrustment, the Central Government may 

itself exercise any of the said functions should it deem fit tp do so in 
any case.

S.O. 3408— In pursuance of clause (1) of article 230 of (he Constitution and in 
supersession of the notification of the Government of India in the Ministry of Home 
Affairs No. l/l/64-(IV)-III, dated the 3rd October, 1904, the President hereby 
iirects that, in relation to the Union territory of Tripura, the District Magistrates, 
West Tripura, North Tripura and South Tripura, within their respective jurio- 
iictions, shall also discharge the functions of the Central Government under the 
Foreigners (Tribunals) Order, 1364, subject to the following conditions, namely: — 

(a) that in the exercise of such functions, the said District Magistrates shall 

comply with such general or special directions as the Chief Com- 
missioner, Tripura, or the Central Government may from time to time 
Issue; and 

(b) that notwithstanding this entrustment, the Central Government way 

Itself exercise any of the said functions should it deem fit to do so in 
any case. 


[No. 1001 1/7/70- (II)-F.III.] 
J. C. AGARWAL, fit. Secy.

MINISTRY OF FINANCE 
(Defence Division) 

(Department of Expenditure) 

New Delhi, the 16 th September 1970 

8.0. 3409. — In exercise of the powers conferred by the proviso to article 309- 
and clause (5) of article 148 of the constitution, and after consultation with the 
Comptroller and Auditor General of India in relation to persons serving in the 
Audit and Accounts Department, the President hereby makes the following regu- 
lations further to amend the Civil Service Regulations, namely; — 

1. (1) These regulations may be called the Civil Service (Fifth Amendment) 
Regulations, 1970. 

(2) They shall come into force on the date of their publication in the Official 
Gazette. 

2. In clause (b) of Article 234 of the Civil Service Regulations, the following 
provisos shall be inserted, namely: — 

"Provided that an officer, who has given notice under clause (i) of Article 
459, or to whom notice has been given under clause (h) of that article, 
may be granted leave due and admissible to pirn, not extending beyond 
the date on which he attains the age of 58 years, or 60 years, if he is 
governed by clause (a), clause (c), or clause (f), as the case may be, 
of the said Art. 459, even though such leave extends beyond the date on 
which he retires on the expiry of the notice under the said Article.” 

Provided further that every officer — 

(a) who, after having been under suspension, is reinstated within six 

months preceding the date of his compulsory retirement and was 
prevented by reason of having been under suspension from applying 
for leave preparatory to retirement, shall be allowed to avail of such 
leave as he was prevented from applying for subject to a maximum 
of six months reduced by the period between the date of reinstate- 
ment and the date of compulsory retirement; 

(b) who retired from service on attaining the age of compulsory retire- 

ment while under suspension and was prevented from applying for 
leave preparatory to retirement on account of having been under 
suspension, shall be allowed to avail of the leave to his credit subject 
to a maximum of six months after the termination of proceedings, 

as if it had been refused as aforesaid if, in the opinion of the authority compe- 
tent to order reinstatement, he has been fully exonerated and the suspension was 
wholly unjustified.” 

[No. F. 7006/AG /PB.J 
K. P. GUPTA, Asstt. Financial Adviser..

CORRIGENDA 

In the Account pursuance tj the Reserve Bank of India Act, 1934 
of the Reserve Bank of India, Issue Department for the week ended the 27th 
February, 1970, published in the Gazette of India, dated 21st March, 1970, at part 
II, Section 3(ii) on page 1421, the figure against the head "Notes in circulation” 
under the liabilities side of the account should be read as 37,55,55,28,000 instead 
of 755,55,28,000.

In the Statement of the Affairs of the Reserve Bank of India Banking Depart- 
ment as on the 27th March 1970, published at page Nos. 1783-84 of Part II, Section 
3(ii) of the Gazette of India dated 11th April 1970, the figure against the head 
“Loans and Advances to (i) Scheduled Commercial Banks” on the assets side should 
be read as 238,02,13,000 instead of 288,02,18,000. 

In the Statement of the Affairs of the Reserve Bank of India, Banking 
Department as on the 24th July, 1970, published at pages 3789-90 of 
part XI, Section 3 (it) of the Government of India Gazette issue dated 29th 
August, 1970, the figure against the head “Loans, Advances and Investments from 
National Agricultural Credit (Long Term Operations) Fund — (a) Loans and 
Advances to: — (li) State Co-operative Banks” on the assets side of the Statement 
should be read as 22,23,69,000 instead of 22,28,69,000.

MINISTRY OF INDUSTRIAL DEVELOPMENT AND INTERNAL TRADE 
(Department of Internal Trade) 

New Delhi, the 6th October 1970 

S.O. 3421. — In exercise of the powers conferred by section 28 of the Forward 
Contracts (Regulation) Act, 1952 (74 of 1952), the Central Government hereby makes 
the following rules further to amend the Forward Contracts (Regulation) [Rules, 
1954, namely: — 

1. These rules may be called the Forward Contracts (Regulation) Amendment 
Rules, 1970. 

2, In the Forward Contracts (Regulation) Rules, 1954, after rule 12, the following 
rule shall be added, namely: — 

“13. Communication of information relating to offence.— The Commission 
shall communicate information in respect of the commission of any 
offence under the Act to the concerned authorities and assist such 
authorities in scrutinising the documents referred to by them and in 
rendering such expert advice as may be required by them". 

[No. F. ll(l)I.T/70.]i 
R. C. SETHI, Dy. Secy.

ORDERS 

New Delhi, the 29th September 1970 

S.O. 3434.— Whereas by the notification of the Government of India in the 
late Ministry of Health No. 16-5/62-MI, dated the 23rd July, 1962, the Central 
Government has directed that the Medical qualification, “M. D.” granted by the 
University of Georgetown, Washington, United States of America, shall be recog- 
nised medical qualification for the purposes of the Indian Medical Council Act, 
1956 (102 of 1956); 

And whereas, Dr. Katherine Florence Job son who possesses the said qualifica- 
tion is for the time being attached to the Holy Family Hospital, Mandar, Ranchi 
District for the purposes of charitable work; 

Now, therefore, In pursuance of clause (c) of the proviso to sub-section (1) of 
section 14 of the said Act, the Central Government hereby specifies— 

(i) a period upto the 31st December, 1970 from the date of publication of 

this order in the Official Gazette, or 

(ii) the period during which Dr. Katherine Florence Jobson, is attached to 

the said Holy Family Hospital, Mandar, Ranchi District, 

whichever is shorter, as the period to which the medical practice by the aforesaid 
doctor shall be limited. 

[No. F. 19-22/70-MPT.]

MINISTRY OF SHIPPING AND TRANSPORT 
(Transport Wing) 

PORTS 

New Delhi, the 29 th September 1970 

8.0. 3437. — In exercise of the powers conferred by the proviso to aitlcle 309 
of the Constitution, the President hereby makes the following rules further to- 




4836 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: OCTOBER 24. 1970/KARTDCA 2. 1892 [Part II— 


amend the Mangalore Harbour Project (Class III and Class IV posts) Recruit- 
ment Rules, 1966, namely: - 

1. (1) These rules may be called the Mangalore Harbour Project (Class IH 
and Class IV posts) Recruitment (Third Amendment) Rules, 1970. 

(2) They shall come into force on the date of their publication in the 
Official Gazette, 

2. For rule 6 of the Mangalore Harbour Project (Class III and Class TV posts) 
Recruitment Rules, 1966, the following rule shall be substituted, namely — 

"6” Disqualification.-— No person — 

(a) who has entered into or contracted a marriage with a person having 

a spouse living, or 

(b) who, having a spouse living, haB entered into or contracted a marriage 

with any person, shall be eligible for appointment to the said 
posts: Provided that the Central Government may, if satisfied that 
such marriage is permissible under the personal law applicable 
to such person and the other party to the marriage and there are 
other grounds for so doing, exempt any person from the operation 
of this rule "

secretary to the Government of Madhy Pradesh, Commerce and Industries Depart- 
ment, Bhopal, to be a member of the Central Silk Board under clause (g) of sub-

(Office of the Chief Controller of Imports and Exports) 

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 9th September 1970 

S O. 3442.— M/s. The Tata Hydro Electric Power Supply Co. Ltd., The Andhra 
Valley Power Supply Co. Ltd,, The Tata Power Co. Ltd., Bombay House. Bruce 
Street, Fort BonXay-1, were granted an import licence No. r|H|2077439|C|XX|32| 
H|29|CG.IV, dated 19th August. 1969, for Rs. 4,388 (Rupees four thousand three 
hundred and eighty-eight only). They have applied for the issue of a duplicate 
Customs Purposes copy of the said licence on the ground that the onginal 
toms Purposes copy has been misplaced. It is fur .her stated that the original 
Customs Purposes copy was registered with the Customs authorities at Bombay 
and was utilised for Rs, 1,333 and the balance available on it was Rs. 3,055. 

2. In support of this contention, the applicant has filed an affidavit duly 
sworn in before Notary Public, Maharashtra State, Bombay, I am accordingly 
satisfied- that the original Customs Purposes copy of the said licence has been 
lost Therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred under Sub-clause 9(cc) of 
the Imports (Control) Order, 1955, dated 7th December 1956 as amended the 
said original Customs Purposes copy of licence No. P|H|2077439, dated 19th 
August, 1969, issued to M|s. Tata Hydro Electric Power Supply Co. Ltd., Bombay, 
is hereby cancelled, 

3. A duplicate Customs Purposes copy of the said licence is being issued sepa- 
rately to the licensee. 


[No. HEP/T-13/69-70/CG.IV.] 
H. D. GUPTA, 

Dy, Chief Controller of Imports and Exports,

Part III 


( 1 ) Medical Facilities: 

(a) Hospitals . — The two Central Hospitals, at Dhanbad and Asansol, and 
11 Regional Hospitals, situated at different parts in the coalfields, continued to 
function during the year under report. Foundation stone was laid on the 18 th 
day of January, 1969 for raising the bed-strength ot the Central Hospital, Dhanbad 
from 300 to 400 by adding an extension wing of 100 beds. In Korea Coalfield, 
the construction of a Central Hospital at Manendragarh with 100 beds was com- 
pleted. Pending commissioning of the Hospital with necessary equipment, action 
was Initiated for starting the hospital as a Regional Hospital with 50 beds. 




4S4 3 TOfe 


GAZETTE 


OF INDIAt OCTOBER 3*. 19?0/&ASftSfcA i, IPjpr tt-' 

_. / 


The proposal for expansion of the Regional Hospitals at Jamai and Nalsarai 
received consideration. The land acquisition proceedings for the Regional 
Hospital, Naisarai were in progress. In Andhra Pradesh Slate Coalfield, the con- 
struction of the Regional Hospital at Ramagundam was completed and necessary 
action to start the functioning of the hospital was taken. The proposals for the 
establishment of Regional Hospitals at Bhurkunda in the Hazaribagh Coalfield, at 
Talcher in the Orissa State Coalfield and at Chanda, Parasia and Bisrampur in 
the Madhya Pradesh State Coalfield were under consideration. Land acquisition 
of 19,85 acres of land in village Kapuria for the Regional Hospital at Kapuria 
and 25.60 acres of land in villages Nunudih and Gharkacha for the township and 
Regional Hospital at Patherdih in the Jharia Coalfield were in progress. As re- 
gards the Regional Hospital at Girmlnt in the West Bengal State Coalfield, 
different sites for establishment of the hospital were selected but as those were 
found to be coal bearing no progress could he made. The West Bengal State 
Coalfield Sub-Committee has again constituted a Site Selection Committee for 
the same purpose. Their report was yet to be received. An amount of 
Rs. 10,42,540 for the construction of the Regional Hospital at Chlnakuri was 
approved by the West Bengal State Coalfield Sub-Committee on the 24th January, 
1960. Delivery of possession of land for the Regional Hospital at Pandaveshwar 
and for the Regional Hospital at Parhelia In the State of West Bengal was yet 
to be received. 


(b) Allopathic Dispensaries. — -The three static allopathic dispensaries one each 

at Mugnm in the Jharia Coalfield, at new selected Dhorl Colliery in the Hazari- 
zagh, Coalfield and at Bhara in the Raniganj Coalfield continued to render ser- 
vice during the year under report. An estimate amounting to Rs. 4,68,820/- for 
the conversion of the dispensary at Mugma into an 18 bedded Regional Hospital 
was sent to the Central Government for approval. , 

(c) Ayurvedic Dispensaries, — Twenty-eight Ayurvedic Dispensaries were 
functioning in the various coalfields. The proposals for the establishment of 
more such dispensaries, one each in Jharia and Andhra Pradesh State Coal- 
fields and 3 in Madhya Pradesh State Coalfields were in different stages of con- 
sideration. For the manufacture of genuine medicines required for the use at 
the Ayurvedic Dispensaries, the Ayurvedic Pharmacy set up by the Coal Mines 
Labour Welfare Organisation at Patherdih in the Jharia Coalfield continued to 
render service during the year under report. 

(d) Family Welfare, Maternity and Child Welfare Centre. — Family Welfare 
Centres attached to each of the Regional Hospitals continued to function during 
the year under report Besides, 8 such Centres established in the various coal- 
fields were also functioning as independent units, each under the charge of a 
Lady Health Visitor, In addition, 53 Maternity and Child Welfare Centres were 
being run by the Asansol, Jharia and Hazaribagh Mines Boards of Health for 
which the Coal Mines Labour Welfare Organisation continued to pay grants-in- 
aid towards their maintenance. 

(e) Financial assistance for improving Dispensary Services. — With a view to 
encouraging the colliery managements to improve the standard of dispensary 
services at the collieries for the benefit of the colliery workers and their depen- 
dents, the scheme for the payment of grants-in-aid was continued and a sum of 
Rs. 12,54,638.05 was paid, during the year, 

(f) Anti-T.B. Measures. — A total number of 262 beds (12 at Katras, 100 each 
at Dhanbad and Kalla and 50 at Searsole) were made available in different hos- 
pitals for the treatment of T.B. affected Colliery workers and dependents. In 
addition to this 77 beds were reserved in various sanatoria upto 31st January 
196T Thereafter the number of beds reserved was reduced to 72 in view of the 
increased facility available at the hospitals of Coal Mines Labour Welfare Orga- 
nisation. 


The work on the construction of a T.B. Clinic with 30 beds at Ramagundam 
in Andhra Pradesh State Coalfields was nearing completion. The construction 
of the T.B, Clinic buildings at Jamai and Kurasia was in progress. The construc- 
tion of the T B. Wing with 50 beds attached to the Central Hospital at Manendra- 
gnrh in Madhya Pradesh State Coalfield was completed. As regards construction 
of the T.B, Clinic at Regional Hospital, Dhanpuri, necessary estimate was sent 
to the Central Government for approval, Central Government approval in con- 
nection with the construction of the T.B, Clinics attached to Regional Hospitals

at Naisarai and'Phusro was received. As regards construe i'on of the T.B. Clinic 
attached to the Regional Hospital at Tisra, the Advisory Committee decided to 
keep in abeyance the said construction as the newly constructed T.B. Clinic at 
Katras, might meet the increasing demand. 

(g) X-ray facilities.— X-ray plants were installed at the Central Hospitals at 
Dhanbad and Asansol and also at some of the Regional Hospitals. Besides, the 
Coal Mines Labour Welfare Organisation also supplied x-ray plants for installa- 
tion at Hospitals run by the colliery managements for the benefit of the colliery 
workers and their dependents. Under this Scheme, 15 x-ray plants have so 
far been supplied and are in commission in various colliery hospitals. 

(h) Treatment of Leprosy. — 54 beds were maintained for the treatment of 
leprosy cases at 3 leprosy hospitals in the States of Bihar and West Bengal. The 
scheme of payment of diet and subsistence allowances on the same line as in the 
case of T.B. patients continued to be in operation. 

(i) Treatment of Mental Cases. — Arrangements existed for the treatment of 
colliery workers and their dependents suffering from mental diseases at the 
Mental Hospitals, at Ranchi and Nagpur. 12 beds at the Mental Diseases Hos- 
pital, Ranchi were also reserved during the year under report for treatment of 
colliery workers and their dependents. 

( j ) Treatment of Cancer. — For the treatment of cancer patients, the deep X- 
ray plant already installed at the Central Hospital, Kalla, Asansol continued to 
render service during the year under report. In addition to the 2 beds which 
had been reserved at the Chittaranjan Cancer Hospital, Calcutta, 12 beds were 
reserved at the Patna Medical College Hospital. Arrangements already existed 
for treatment of cancer patients at this Hospital. 

(k) Rehabilitation. — Rehabilitation-cum-Physiotherapy Centre attached to the 
Central Hospitals at Dhanbad and Asansol coninued to function. The construc- 
tion of the Rshabilitation-cum-Conva^escent Horne, Home, for the colliery workers of 
Bihar State and West Bengal State Coalfields at Sidbabari near Chittaranjan, 
is in progress. A sum of Rs. 6,372 was sanctioned to M/s. Assam Rly. & Trading 
Co. Ltd. for construction of a Rehabilitation-cum-G.vrpaaa&iuni Centre at Mar- 
gherita and purchase of equipments therefor. The management reported com- 
pletion of the building. An ad-hoc non-recurring grant of Rs. 50,000 was paid 
as padt payment to the Maria Rehabilitation Centre in return for their under- 
taking to supple on priority basis artificial limbs tc disabled coal miners spon- 
sored by the Coal Mines Labour Welfare Organisation. 


Foundation stone was laid on 2nd October, 1968 for establishment of a Reha- 
bilitation centre at Chhindwara by the Union Minister lor Labour and Rehabiii- 
tion. Necessary ae'icn for starting the construction of the Centre was taken. 

(1) Family Planning . — Free advice on family planr,:-' r - continued 10 be given 
and contraceptives were supplied free of cost, at the hospitals run under the 
financial help from the Fund. Family Planning Clinics attached to each cf the 
Central Hospitals also continued to funct : nn during the year. Gome urban and 
rural clinics were also opened. The scheme for the payment of grants-in-aid 
to the colliery owners for the family planning work continued to be in opera- 
tion. The Scheme for setting uo of 5 static Family Planning Units. one each at 
the Central Hospital" at Dhanbad and Asansol and three at the Regional Hospitals 
at Naisarai in the Hazaribagh Coalfield and at Jamai and Dhannuri in the 
Madhya Pradesh State, Coalfield, sanctioned during the year 1967-68. was being 
implemented. The setting up of 3 mobile family planning units, one each attach- 
ed to the Central Hospitals at Dhanbad and Asansol and to the Regional Hospital 
at Bhuli. was sanctioned during 1967-68. These units started functioning with 
the recruitment of necessary staff.

ib) Games and Sports '. — During the year, for organising various games and sports, a sum of 
Rs. 2,31, 363/-was sanctioned. 

[i) On the 24th November, 1968 the 8th All India Coalfield Foot-ball tournament was 
held at Kurasia Colliery n Madhya Pradesh State Coalfield. Shrl S. C. Jamir, Deputy 
Minister for Labour, Employment and Rehabilitation, Government of India graced 
the occasion as Chief Gu st and gave away the prizes. The Jahria Coalfield Team, 
won the said tournament and the Hazanbagh Coalfield Team was the runners up. 

(ii) The Raniganj Coalfield Sports was held In Smala Mandarboni Colliery 
on the 18th January. 1969. Shri Jaisukhlal Hathi, Union Minister for Labour, 
Employment and Rehabilitation was the Chief Guest. Shri, S. N. Pande, Coal 
Mines Welfare Commissioner presided. The Union Minister highly appreciated 
the high standard of discipline and performance of the athelets. Athelets from 
all the six Zones of Raniganj Coalfield participated in the sports in which the 
Bahula Zone won the team championship. 

(iii) The Jharia Coalfield Sports for 1968-09 was held in Kustore Colliery 
Ground on the 12th January, 1969. Athelets from the six Zones of the Jharia 
Coalfield participated in the sports. The Championship wars won by the Katras 
Zone. 

(iv) The Hazarihagh Coalfield Sports was held on the 16th December, 1969 
in Pipradih Colliery Ground. Athelets from all the three zones namely, Ram gar h, 
Bokaro and Giridih participated in the sports. The Ramgarh Zone won the 
Championship. 

(v) The All India Coalfield Volley Ball tournament was held from the 19th 
to 23rd March, 1969 at Moira Colliery Stadium where all the 8 coalfields teams 
participated. The final game was played between the teams of Madhya Pradesh 
State and Andhra Pradesh State Coalfields in which the Andhra Pradesh State 
Coalfield team was defeated. 

(vi) The year 1969 marks the silver jubilee year of the Coal Mines Labour 
Welfare Organisation. The 8th All India Coalfields sports were held in Jealgora 
Colliery Stadium on 21st and the 22nd February, 1C69 in commemoration of the 
silver jubilee. About 210 competitors from the Coalfield areas all over India 
participated in the sports. The team championship was won by Raniganj Coal- 
field. The Jharia Coalfield Team was the runners up. The individual champion- 
ship open to all, was won by Shri Harimider Singh of Jharia Coalfield, Shri 
Jaisukh Lai Hathi, Union Minister for Labour, Employment and Rehabilitation 
witnessed the sports as Chief Guest. Shri R. K, Malviya, Chairman, Committee 
on Labour Welfare and former Deputy Lkbour Minister presided. 

(c) Bharat Danhan Special Train . — The 10th Bharat Darshon Yaltra Special 
Train which left Dhanbad on the 4th September, 1968 on a North India tour 
carrying about 600 coal miners returned to Dhanbad in the 3rd week of Septem- 
ber, 1968 after visiting important religious and other places of interest, Similarly 
the 11th Bharat Darshan Yatra Special Train carrymg about the same number 
of miners started on the 4th December, 1908 on a South India Tour and returned 
to Dhanbad on the 1st January, 1969. During the year, 39 colliery workers of 
Andhra Pradesh State went on tour to Kashmir; an expenditure of Rs. 9,000 was 
incurred in this connection.

(3) Welfare Activities : 

(a) Water Supply. — A brief review of the progress made in respect of the 
various water supply schemes in the different coalfields during the year under 
report is given below: — 

(1) Jharia and Magma Coalfield. — Darnodar Water Supply Schemes. — The 
Scheme was in progress. 

Water Supply Scheme at Nudidih. — The scheme was nearing completion. 
Necessary action for making Anal payment amounting to Rs. 24,676/- has been 
taken, 

Water Supply Scheme at Katras— The scheme was nearing completion. 
Necessary action for making final payment amounting to Rs. 21,693 has been 
taken. 


Water Supply Scheme at Loyahad Colliery. —The scheme was nearing comple- 
tion. Necessary action for making final payment amounting to Rs. 6009/- has 
been taken. 

Water Supply Scheme at Monidih.—i The scheme was under execution. Public 
Health Engineer of the Organisation recommended payment of Rs, 28183/- 
represenlting 25 per cent of the initial subsidy to the collieries. The collieries 
were requested to complete the formalities, required in this connection. 

Water Supply Scheme at North Tetulmarl Colliery. — The Scheme submitted by 
M/s. North Tetulmari Colliery estimated to cost Rs, 20,775/- was forwarded to 
the Jharia Coalfield Sub-Committee for consideration. 


Water Supply Scheme at Chanch— The Scheme estimated to cost Rs. 1,52,571.05 
was completed, Necessary action for final payment of Rs. 44,025 as subsidy has 
been taken, 


Water Supply Scheme at Laikdih Deep Colliery. — The Scheme estimated to cost 
Rs. 99,772/- was completed and a sum of Rs. 24,993/- as full and final payment 
was paid. 

(ii) Ramgarh-Karanpura Coalfield — The Integrated water supply scheme at 
south Karanpura Group of Collieries was completed. The estimated cost of the 
scheme was Rupees one crore. 20 per cent of the estimated cost was paid. 
Necessary action for final payment has been taken. 

(ilil Bokaro Coalfield.— The Integrated water supply scheme at Bokaro and 
Kargali was completed. 20 per cent of the estimated cost was paid. Necessary 
action for final payment has been taken. 

(iv) West Bengal Coalfield. — Integrated water supply scheme at Raniganj . — 
The scheme was to be implemented in three stages. First stage of the scheme 
was started. A sum of Rs. 21 lakhs had been paid during the year over and above 
Rs. 39,00,000/- paid during previous year, .. , , . 

Water Supply Schemes at Chlnakur and Parhelia. — These schemes estimated to 
cost Rs- 1,93,367.00 and Rs. 1,99,746-00 respectively were completed and necessary 
action for final payment has been taken, 


Water Supply Scheme at Sarnia Monderbani.— The Public ealth Enginer of 
the Organisation recommende payment of Rs. 46.039/- representing 25 per cent 
of the estimated cost to the colliery company which was being paid, 

(v) Madhya Pradesh Coalfield.— The scheme for water supply at Birsingpur 
colliery estimated to cost Rs, 1,05,324/- was technically sanctioned and a sum of 
Rs. 41,331/- was recommended for payment as an initial instalment of subsidy. 

Sinking of wells. — 17 wells were sanctioned during the year for construction in 
different coalfields with financial assistance to the extent of 50 per cent by way 
of subsidy from the Coal Mines Labour Welfare Fund Organisation The total 
number of wells sanctioned so far were 431. 21 wells were completed during 

the year raising the total number of the wells constructed so far to 216. A sum 
of Rs. 64,800/- has been paid to the different colliery owners as subsidy m this 

P ° n f cT ^Cooperative Movement Organisation.— During the year no new primary 
stores credit cooperative societies and Wholesale or Central Cooperative Stores 
were registered. The total number of credit cooperative societies primary stores 

and Wholesale or Central Cooperative Stores functioning were 197, 374 and 12 

reS lSinJ y the year under renort. the Government sanctioned a sum of Rs 15 
lakhs for advancing loan to the colliery workers members of credit cooperative

S.O, 3446. — In exercise of the powers conferred by sub— section (1) of section 4 
of the Dock Workers (Regulation of Employment) Act^ 1948 (8 of 1948), the 
Central Government hereby makes the following further amendment in the Madras 
Unregistered Dock Workers (Regulation of Employment) Scheme, 1957, the sauna 
having been previously published as required by the said sub-section, namely: — 

1. This Scheme may be called the Madras Unregistered Dock Workers 

(Regulation of Employment). Amendment Scheme, 1970. 

2. In the Madras Unregistered Dock Workers (Regulation of Employment) 

Scheme, 1957, clause 6-B shall be renumbered as sub-clause (1) there- 
of and after sub-clause (1) so renumbered, tho following sub-clauses 
shall be inserted, namely: — 


"(2) (a) The Administrative Body shall keep separate accounts of the 
receipts of levy and expenses including the payments like dis- 
appointment money, attendance allowance, guaranteed minimum 
wages, Provident Fund, or gratuity in respect of each of the three 
pools of workers covered by clause 9 A of the Scheme. For the 
purposes of the accounting the receipts and expenditure, each of 
tho said three pools shall be treated as a sepurate and distinct unit 
namely coal, ore and chipping and painting. The receipts from one 
unit shall not be utilised to meet the expenses of any other unit 
except with the prior permission and sanction in writing of the 
Madras Dock Labour Board. 


(b) 


(3) 


The Administrative Body shall allocate the overhead expenses in- 
curred by It for administering the Scheme to each of the units,, 
separately in proportion to the receipts from each of the units. 

The Administrative Body shall arrange to see that the amounts ex- 
pended from the receipt of one unit for any other unit and r ™, J 1 ' 
W unpaid on the date when the aforesaid clause is brought into 
operation is immediately recovered from the concerned listed 
employer or employers, and failure on the part of the concerned 
listed employer to pay such amounts will attract the provisions of 
clause 11 A of the Scheme without prejudice to any other mode of 
recovery of the said amounts.

S.O. 3450. — In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 
of 1947), the Central Government hereby publishes the following award of the 
Central Government Industrial Tribunal, Calcutta, In the industrial dispute between 
the employers in relation to the management of Methani Colliery of Messrs Equit- 
able Coal Company Limited, Post Office Sitarampur, District Burdwan and their 
workmen, which was received by the Central Government on the 5th October, 
1970. 


CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL. CALCUTTA 


Reference No. 28 of 1970 


Parties 

Employers in relation to the management of Methani Colliery of Messrs- 
Equitable Coal Company Limited, 


And

On behalf of Workmen — Sri T. Chattaraj, Secretary, Colliery Mazdoor- 
Congress (H.M.S.). 

State : West Bengal Industry : Coal Mines 

AWARD 

By Order No. 6/22/70-LRII, dated July 7, 1970, the Government of India, in 
the Ministry of Labour, Employment and Rehabilitation (Department of Labour 
and Employment) referred the following industrial dispute between the employers 
in relation to the management of Methani Colliery of Messrs Equitable Coal 
Company Limited and their workmen, to this Tribunal, for adjudication, namely: 
“Whether the promotion of Shri Santosh Dey as E'espatch Clerk Grade I and 
Shri Amarnath Mukherjee as Assistant Despatch Clerk Grade H has 
been deliberately and un-jusliftably delayed and if so, to whpt relief, 
are they entitled?” 


2. Both the management and the workmen filed their respective written state- 
ment. I am, however, not called upon to go into the dispute because to-day, which 
was fixed for peremptory hearing, the parties filed a joint petition of compromise, 
settling ihelr dispute on certain terms. Now, that the parties have settled their 



-4858 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: OCTOBER 24, 1970/KARTIKA 2 , 1892 [Part II— 


dispute, I dispose of the Reference on terms of settlement. Let the petition of 
settlement form part of this award. 

Dated, September 29, 1970. (Sd.) B. N. Banerjee, 

Presiding Oificer. 


BEFORE THE PRESIDING OFFICER, CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL 

TRIBUNAL, CALCUTTA 

Reference No. 28 of 1970 


Parties : 

Employers in relation to the Management of Methani Colliery of Messrs 
Equitable Coal Co. Ltd., 

And 

Their Workinen. 

The parties above named jointly beg to state: — 

1. That the parties have agreed to settle the dispute under reference amicably 
on terms set-out below: — 


Terms 

(a) That the workman Shri Santosh Day will be promoted as Despatch Clerk 

Grade I with effect from 15th August 1971 without any retrospective 
claim what-so-ever. 

(b) That the workman Shri Amar Nath Mukherjee will have his grievance 

mutually settled by discussion with the Management and as such does 
not press his claim for adjudication in the instant reference. 

2. That the workmen will have no other claim against the Management in 
respect of the subject matter of the reference. 

3. That the parties will bear their respective cost of this reference. 

It is, therefore, prayed that the Hon’ble Tribunal be phased to permit the 
parlies to settle the dispute on the above terms and to pass an Award accordingly 
by treating this petition as a part of the Award. 

('Sd.) Illegible 

Secretary. Principal Officer. 

Colliery Mazdoor Congress (K.l.I.S), ,'or Employers: 

Branch: Methari Colliery. Equitable Coal Co., Ltd. 

For Workmen. (Sd.) R. H. Wright, 

Dated: 29th September, 1970. Managing Director. 

(Sd.) B. P. Banerjee, 

Labour Officer,

S.O. 3451. — In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 
of 1947), the Central Government hereby publishes the following award of Shri 
M. R. Raju, Arbitrator in the industrial dispute betiveen the employers in rela- 
tion to the management of Bhilai Steel Plant, Bhilai and their workmen, which 
~was received by the Central Government on the 5th October, 1970. 




SEC. 3(il)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: OCTOBER ?4, 1970/KARTIKA 2. 1892 4859 


nf Hhiu? ? rbi ‘ ra *L on in th f industrial dispute between the Management 

° ? • a 1 anc * ^eir workmen regarding removing from services £er- 

R»m ri lW« Singh, G ° k ui Ram, Pancho 0 , Ajar Das, Biharoo, Ram Kishan, Bhfgat 
Kam, Dewar Chand and Prem Lai. 


Rresent : 

Shri M. R. Raju, 

Regional Labour Commissioner (Central), 
and Arbitrator. 


Representing Employer: 

Shri R. P. Singh, 

Sr. Industrial Relations Officer. 

Shri K. G. Marar,

Shri P. K. Jha, 

Additional Labour Welfare Officer. 

Shri S. D. Dikshit,

Shri A. R. Farooqui, 

President, Bhilai Steel Kamgar Sangh. 

Shri Deo Saran Dubey, 

General Secretary, Bhilai Steel Kamgar Sangh 
No. RLC (V)/2/70 


AWARD 

The management of Bhilai Steel Plant, Bhilai on the one hand and the Bhilai 
Steel Kamgar Sangh, Bhilai on the other, entered into a settlement on 6 th 
January, 1970, agreeing to refer the following industrial dispute for my arbitra- 
tion under Section 10-A of the Industrial Act, 1947: — 

“Whether the action of the management of Bhilai Steel Plant in removing 
S/Shri Amar Singh, Gokul Ram Panchoo, Ajardas, Biharoo, Ram 
Kishan, Bhagat Ram, Dewar and Prem Lai from service from the 
establishment of Chandi Dongrj Mines is proper and justified? If 
not, to what relief are these workmen entitled having regard to the 
possible repercussions in industry?” 

2. The parties further agreed that the arbitrator shall make his award within 
six months from the date of settlement. It was further stipulated in the Agree- 
ment that in case the Award was not made within a period of six months from 
the date of settlement or within such further time as extended by mutual agree- 
ment between them in writing the reference to arbitration should stand automati- 
cally cancelled and they would be free to negotiate for fresh arbitration. The afore- 
said settlement making me arbitrator was published in the Gazette of India 
dated the 14th March, 1970. The period of six monlths agreed to between the 
parties in the earlier settlement had expired on the 5th July, 1970, but the parties 
agreed on 4th July 1970 to extend the time limit for giving my award by a fur- 
ther period of three months. 

3. After obtaining statements of their case from the parties concerned with 
this reference. I took up the case for hearjng at Bhilai on 8 th June 1970. On this 
date, the parties requested time for filing documents to be sent by Registered 
Post by 20tb June and exchange the same between them. It was further agreed 
that the hearing will be taken up at Delhi on 25th June, 1970. On this date none 
appeared for the hearing and the management requested for adjournment and the 
Union was informed of the same bv them. Subsequently dates were fixed for 
hearing at Delhi on 12th August 1970, 31st August, 22nd September 1970 and 1st 
October 1970, and on every occasion the management requested for adjournment. 
It appears that the parties have entered into mutual discussions after the first 
date of hearing on 8 lh June 1970, with a view to settle the matter and therefore 
were seeking adjournments. Tire parties have not filed the relevant documents 
as agreed bv them on 8 th June 1970. The Union submitted only a copy of the 
judgement of the Additional Tahasildar and Magistrate 2nd Class Ralnandgaon In 
the Criminal Case No. 14 of 1903 which I had no means to verify the correctness 
in the absence of the original, and no other documents. The management had 
not filed any document and not even the copies of enquiry proceedings. 



4 S$0 THU GAZETTE OF INDIA: OCTOBER 24, 1970/KARTIKA 2, 1892 [Part II— 


4. It would thus be seen that the Union appears (to be more interested in pur- 
suing the case with the management by direct negotiations rather than pursuing 
it before the arbitrator. In (the circumstances it is not possible for me to wait 
indefinitely for the parties to conclude the mutual discussions, Moreover the 
extended period of time will expire on the 4th October, 1970. Hence it was not 
possible for me to grant any further adjournment on the requgst of the manage- 
ment, when there was no communication whatsoever from the Union seeking 
adjournment on the last date of hearing on 1st October 1970, In addition to this 
the extended time which expires on 4th has also to be extended by both the 
contending parties which has also not been done. On consideration of all these 
circmstances, it is neither possible for me to decide the issue on its merits nor 
to drag on with this case further. 1, therefore, hereby give no ward. 

(Sd.) M. R. Raju, 

Regional Labour Commissioner (Central) 
and Arbitrator. 

[No. 37 (33) /69-LRIV.]

New Delhi, the 12th October 1970 

S O. 3455. — Whereas an Industrial dispute exists between the employers in 
relation to the management of Pyrites, Phosphates and Chemicals Limited, Post 
Office Amjhore, District Shahabad (Bihar) and their workmen represented by Elect- 
ed Representative of the workers of Pyrites, Phosphates and Chemicals Limited, 
Post Office Amjhore, District Shahabad (Bihar). 

And whereas the said employers and their workmen have by a written agree- 
ment. in pursuance of the provisions of sub-section (1) of section 10A of the Indus- 
trial -Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), referred the said dispute to the arbitration 
by the persons specified therein, and a copy of the said arbitration agreement has 
been forwarded to the Central Government; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of the provisions of sub-section (3) of section 10A 
Of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government hereby 
publishes the said arbitration agreement. 



Sec. 3(ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: OCTOBER 24, 1970/KARTIKA 2, 1892 4863 


Agreement 

(Under Section 10-A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947) 

Between 


Name of the Parties: 

(b) Representing the workmen. 

1. Shrl K. C. Das. 

2. Shri J. Narayan. 

3. Shri Md. Lukman. 

4. Shri Sobrati Ansarl. 

5. Shri P. N. Dubey. 

8. Shri P. K. Mukherjee. 

7. Shri Ashok Kumar. 

8, Shri Ramaewak Singh, 

8, Shri Shyam Murari Prasad, 

10. Shri B. Pandey. 

o refer the following industrial dis- 
pute to the arbitration of Shri Ramchor Prasad, Chairman and Managing Director, 
N.C.D.C. Ltd., Darbhanga House, Ranchi. 

I. Specific matters in dispute: 

(i) Whether the existing wage structure of the various categories of the daily- 
rated workmen of the Pyrites mines at Amjhore is adequate? 

(.li) If not, what should be the proper wage structure taking into considera- 
tion the special feature of pyrites mining, impact of the wage struc- 
ture so evolved on the cost of production and the desirability of the 
system of payments by results? 

(iii) Whether any part of the wages should be linked with the cost of living 

index, taking into consideration the workers' demand for a Canteen 
at the work-site to supply meals at the subsidised rates? and If so, 
what should be its rates? 

(iv) What should be the period of operation of the award of the Arbitrator? 

II. Details of the parties to the dispute Including the name and address of the 
establishment or undertaking: 

(a) Management of Pyrites, Phosphates and Chemicals Ltd., P.O. Amjhore, 

DIst Shahabad, (Bihar) and 

(b) 10 workers representing the daily-rated workers of P.P.C. Ltd. 

1. Shri K. C. Das. 

*2. Shri J. Narayan. 

3. Shri Md. Lukman. 

4. Shri Sobrati Ansarl. 

5. P. N. Dubey. 

6. Shri P. K. Mukherjee. 

7. Shri Ashok Kumar. 

8. Shri Ramsewak Singh. 

9. Shri Shyam Murari Prasad. 

10. Shri B. Pandey, 

III. Name of the Union, if any, representing the workmen in question: 

Nil. 

IV. Total No. of workmen employed in the Undertaking affected: 

2173 (approx.). 

V. No. of the workmen affected or likely to be affected by the dispute: 

1800 daily-rated (approx). 

We further agree that th e decision of the Arbitrator shall be binding on us 
and that the Arbitrator shall give his award in two phases, i.e. 

(a> For the period from 1st June;, 1968 to 31st July, 1970 taking into consi- 
deration the ad hoc increase allowed from 1st June, 1908. 


(a) Representing the Employers 

1. Shri T, N. Jaggi Chief Mining 
Engineer. 

2. Shri S. Nagarajan, F.A. & C-A.O. 
3. Shri S. S. GiU, M. M, (Mines;. 


It is hereby agreed between the parties 



4864 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA; OCTOBER 24, 1970/KARTIKA 2, 1892 [Part II— 


(b) From 1st August, 1970 onwards, taking Into consideration the interim 
relief of Rs. 16/- p,m, being given from 1st August 1970. 


The Arbitrator shall give his ward within a period of 3 months or within such 
further time as extended by mutual agreement in writing. In case the award is 
not made within the period aforesaid the reference to the arbitrator shall stand 
automatically cancelled and we shall be free to negotiate for fresh arbitration. 
Management’s representatives: Representatives of the daily-rated workers:

Government hereby refers the said dispute for adjudication to the Industrial 
Tribunal, Jabalpur, constituted under section 7A of the said Act.

S.O. 3460. — Whereas the Central Government Is of opinion that an industrial 
dispute exists between the employers in relation to the management of South 
Bulliary Colliery of Messrs East Indian Coal Company Limited, Post Office 
Kusunda, District Dhanbad and their workmen In respect of the matters specified 
in the Schedule hereto annexed; 

And whereas the Central Government considers it desirable to refer the said 
dispute for adjudication; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by clause (d) of sub-sec- 
tion (1) of section 10 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby refers the said dispute for adjudication to the Central Govern- 
ment Industrial Tribunal (No. 2), Dhanbad constituted under section 7A of the 
said Act. 


Schedule 

“Whether the action of the management of South Bulliary Colliery of 
Messrs East Indian Coal Company Limited, Post Office Kusunda, 
District Dhanbad in placing in Category III of the Coal Wage Board’s 
recommendations Sarvashri Housla Prasad, Durga Das and Basdeo 
Misra, Haulage Khalasis is justified? If not, to what relief are these 
workmen entitled and since when?"

S.O. 3462. — Whereas the Central Government is of opinion that an Industrial 
dispute exists between the employers in relation to the Andhra Bank Limited and 
their workmen in respect of the matters specified in the Schedule hereto annexed; 

And, whereas the Central Government considers it desirable to refer the said 
dispute for adjudication; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by section 7A, and clause 
(d) of sub-section (1) of section 10, of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 
1947), the Central Government hereby constitutes an Industrial Tribunal ol which 
Shri T. Chandrasekhara Reddy shall be the Presiding Officer, with headquarters 
at Hyderabad and refers the said dispute for adjudication to the said Tribunal. 

Schedule 

"(i) Whether the demand of the Andhra Bank Employees’ Union, Hyderabad 
that the members of the subordinate staff of the Andhra Bank Limited who have 
passed the matriculation or equivalent examination should be promoted to the 
clerical cadre without any test or interview is justified? If so, to what relief if 
any, are the subordinate staff entitled? 

(ill What should be the criteria and rules for promotion of subordinate staff 
of the Bunk to clerical cadre?" 


[No. 23/121/70-LRIII,]

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 30 th September, 1970 

S.O. 3465. — Whereas an application has been made under section 19(b) of the 
Payment of Bonus Act, 1965 by Messrs North West Coal Co. Ltd. (employer) in 
relation to their establishments mentioned in the Schedule below for extension 
of the period for the payment of bonus to their employees for the accounting year 
ending on 31st December, 1969. 

And whereas being satisfied that there are sufficient reasons to extend the 
time I have, in exercise of the powers conferred on me by the proviso to clause 
(b) of Section 19 of the said Act read with the notification of the Government of 
India in the Ministry of Labour & Employment No. WB-20(42)/65, dated the 28th 
August, 1985, passed orders on 28th September 1970 extending the period for 
payment of the said bonus by the said employer by 4 months (upto 31st December, 
1970) from the last date for payment of bonus under clause (b) of Section 19 oil 
the Act. 

Now this is published for information of the employer and all the employees 
of the said establishment.

(Department of Agriculture) 

New Delhi , the 29 th September 1970 

S.O. 3467. — The following draft rules further to amend the Feni'el (Whole 
and Ground), Fenugreek (Whole and Ground) and Celery seeds (Whole) Grading 
and Marking Rules, 1967 which the Central Government proposes to make in 
exercise of the powers conferred by section 3 of the Agricultural Produce 
(Grading and Marking) Act, 1937 fl of 1937), are published as required by the 
said section for the information of all persons likely to be affected thereby and 
notice is hereby given that the said draft will be taken into consideration on or 
after 5th November, 1970. 

■ Any objections or suggestions which may be received from any person with 
respect to the said draft before the date aforesaid, will be considered by the Cen- 
tral Government. 

Draft Rules 

1. These rules may be called the Fennel (Whole and Ground), Fenugreek 
(Whole and Ground) and Celery seeds (Whole) Grading and Marking (Amend- 
ment) Rules, 1970. 

2. In the Fennel (Whole and Ground), Fenugreek (Whole and Ground) and 
Celery seeds (Whole) Grading and Marking Rules, 1967, 

(f) for rule 4, the following rule shall be substituted, namely: —

Definitions : — 

1 . “Inorganic extraneous matter" means dust, dirt, stones, earth or any other inorganic 
foreign matter. 

2 . "Organic extraneous matter” means chaff, stalks, stems, straw, other seeds or any other 
organic foreign matter. 

3 . "Damaged and blackened seeds” are those fruits which are damaged or blackened materially 
affecting the quality. 

4 . “Insects bored seeds” are those fruits which are partially or wholly bored or eaten by 
wecviles or othsr insects, 

5 . “Shrivelled and immature seeds” are those fruits which are not properly developed.

New Delhi, the 5th October 1970 

S.O. 3468. — The following draft rules further to amend the Coriander Grading 
and Marking Rules, 1964, which the Central Government propose to make in exer- 
cise of the powers conferred by section 3 of the Agricultural Produce (Grading and 
Marking) Act, 1937 (1 of 1937), are published, as required by the said section, for 
the information of all persons likely to be atlected thereby and notice is hereby 
given that the said draft will be taken into consideration on or after 10th November, 
1970. 

Any objection or suggestion which may be received from any person with respect 
to the said draft before the date specified above, will be considered by the Central 
Government. 


Draft Rules 

1. These rules may be called the Coriander Grading and Marking (Amendment) 
Rules, 1970. 

2. In the Coriander Grading and Marking Rules, 1964, for Schedule II, the fol- 
lowing Schedule shall be substituted, namely: —

Definitions '. —

S.O. 3469. — Whereas, the Central Government has, in pursuance ol clause (d) 
of sub-section (1) of section 5 of the Prevention of Cruelty to Animals Act, 196# 
(59 of 1960), nominated Dr. T. K. Thanewala, Deputy Director General (H.A.) to be 
a member of the Animal Welfare Board nice Dr. Y. K. Subramanlyam; 

And whereas, the Municipal Corporation of Calcutta has, In pursuance of clause 
(e) of sub-section (1) of section 5 of the said Act, nominated Dr. S. Bagchi as Its 
representative on the said Board vice Dr. G, S. Das; 


SEC. 3(il)J THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: OCTOBER 24, 1970/KARTIKA 2, 1892 4885 


Now, therefore. In pursuance of sub-section (1) of section 4, read with section 5 
of the said Act, the Central Government hereby makes the following further amend- 
ments in the notification of the Government of India In the late Ministry of Food 
and Agriculture (Department of Agriculture) No. S.O. 921, dated the 20th March, 
1962, namely: — 

In the said notification — 

(a) in item 4, for the entry in the first column, the following entry shall be 

substituted namely: — 

“Dr. T, K. Thanewala, Deputy Director-General (H-A.), Directorate General 
of Health Services, New Delhi.”; 

(b) In item 7, for the entry in the first column, the following entry shall be 

substituted, namely: — 

“Dr. S. Bagchi, Acting Health Officer, Corporation of Calcutta, 5, S. N. 
Bannerjee Road, Calcutta-13”.

S.O. 3473. — Whereas the Election Commission is satisfied that Shri Okram 
Rabei Singh, Khongman Moirang, Pandit Leikai (Manipur), a contesting candi- 
date for election to the Manipur Legislative Assembly from Thongju Constituen- 
cy. has failed to lodge an account of his; election expenses as required by the 
Representation of the People Act, 1951, and the Rules mode thereunder; 



4888 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: OCTOBER 24, 1970/KARTIKA 2, 1892 [Part II 


2. And whereas, the said candidate even after due notice has not given any 
reason or explanation for the failure and the Election Commission is satisfied 
that he has no good reason or justification for such failure; 

3. Now, therefore, in pursuance of section 10A of the said Act, the Election 
Commission hereby declares the said Shrl Okram Robei Singh to be disqualified 
lor being chosen as and for being a member of either House of Parliament or 
©f the Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council of a State for a period of 
three years from the date of this order.

S.O. 3474. — Whereas the Election Commission is satisfied that Shri Kharapat, 
S/o Shri Swaroop, R/o village Amara, Post Office, Chakia, District Varanasi, 
Uttar Pradesh, a contesting candidate for mid-term general election 1969, to the 
Uttar Pradesh Legislative Assembly from 244-Chaltla (SC) Assembly Constituen- 
cy, has failed to lodge an account of his election expenses as required by the 
Representation of the People Act, 1951, and the Rules made thereunder; 

2. And, whereas, the said candidate even after due notices has not given 
any reason or explanation for the failure and the Election Commission is satisfied 
that he has no good reason or justification for such failure; 

3, Now, therefore, In pursuance of section 10A of the said Act, the Election 
Commission hereby declares the said Shri Kharapat, to be disqualified for being 
chosen as and for being a member of either House of Parliament or of the Legis- 
lative Assembly or Legislative Council of a State for a period of three years 
from the date of this order.

MINISTRY OF FINANCE 
(Department of Revenue and Insurance) 
(Central Excise Collectorate, Baroda) 

Customs 


Baroda, the 16 th September 1970 

S.O. 3482. — In exercise of the powers conferred by clause (a) of Section 8 of 
the Customs Act, 1962 (52 of 1962), I, the Collector of Customs & Central Excise, 
Baroda, hereby make the following amendment in Notification No. 117/50 dated 
0th September, 1950 issued by the late Central Board of Revenue, New Delhi, 
namely. 

In the Schedule annexed to the said Notification, against Port Okha, in Column 
3 after entry No. 6, the following entry shall be inserted 
“7. Dry Cargo Berth for all goods". 


[No. 4.] 
L. M. K1AUL, Collector
